You said: What is the Kannada or Sanskrit word that uses ೠ - Does any word start with this letter? Why is it included in the aksharamaale.

Copilot said:

Short answer

ೠ is the Kannada-script form of the Sanskrit vowel ॠ, transliterated ṝ. It is called:

ದೀರ್ಘ ಋ in Kannada
dīrgha ṛ in Sanskrit grammatical terminology
long vocalic r in linguistics

It is not a normal Kannada vowel, and practically no ordinary Kannada word begins with ೠ. It is included in extended versions of the Kannada ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ because the Kannada script has historically also been used to write Sanskrit.

ಋ versus ೠ
Independent letter	Vowel sign	Transliteration	Descriptionಋ	ೃ	ṛ	Short vocalic r
ೠ	ೄ	ṝ	Long vocalic r

For example:

ಕೃ = kṛ
ಕೄ = kṝ

The Unicode name of ೠ, code point U+0CE0, is “Kannada Letter Vocalic RR.” It is categorized as an additional Kannada-script vowel used for Sanskrit.

Does any word begin with ೠ?
In Kannada

As far as normal Kannada vocabulary is concerned, no ordinary native word begins with ೠ. In fact, the sound ṝ is not part of the regular Kannada vowel system. Even ಋ in Kannada occurs largely in Sanskrit-derived words such as:

ಋಷಿ — ṛṣi, sage
ಋತು — ṛtu, season
ಋಣ — ṛṇa, debt
ಋಗ್ವೇದ — Ṛgveda

Modern Kannada pronunciation usually realizes ಋ approximately as ರು / ri / ru, depending on the speaker and word. The original Sanskrit sound is a syllabic r, meaning that the r itself functions as the vowel.

In Sanskrit

There are dictionary and grammatical listings in which ॠ / ೠ itself is treated as a sound, interjection, grammatical item or symbolic lexical entry. However, ordinary Sanskrit lexical words essentially do not begin with long ṝ.

The Monier-Williams Sanskrit-English Dictionary gives entries for ॠ, including its use as the name of the eighth vowel and some highly specialized meanings. These are dictionary or grammatical entries, not normal everyday words beginning with the vowel.

So the careful answer is:

ೠ can technically occur independently, but there is no familiar, commonly used Kannada or Sanskrit word that naturally begins with it.

Where is the sound actually used?

The long vowel ṝ occurs mainly inside inflected Sanskrit words, especially in certain grammatical forms of nouns ending in-ṛ.

For example:

Sanskrit in Kannada script	IAST	Meaningಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್	pitṝṇām	of the fathers
ಮಾತೄಣಾಮ್	mātṝṇām	of the mothers
ಪಿತೄನ್	pitṝn	the fathers, accusative plural
ಮಾತೄಃ	mātṝḥ	mothers, certain plural forms

Here ೠ is not used, because the vowel follows a consonant. Instead, its dependent vowel sign ೄ is attached to the preceding consonant:

ತ + ೄ = ತೄ
ಪಿತೃ → ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್

The long ṝ generally appears in specific inflectional forms of nouns whose stems end in short ṛ, particularly certain plural case forms.

Then why is ೠ included in the ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ?

There are several related reasons.

1. Kannada script was also a script for Sanskrit

The distinction between language and script is important:

Kannada is a language.
Kannada script is a writing system.
That writing system has also been used to write Sanskrit, as well as Tulu, Konkani and some other languages.

Therefore, the full Kannada-script inventory includes characters necessary for representing Sanskrit sounds, even when those sounds are absent from native Kannada.

2. Sanskrit grammar recognizes short and long vowels

Traditional Sanskrit phonology classifies most simple vowels into short and long counterparts:

ಅ / ಆ
ಇ / ಈ
ಉ / ಊ
ಋ / ೠ

Thus ೠ is the theoretically and grammatically corresponding long form of ಋ. Sanskrit sources classify ṛ as short and ṝ as its long counterpart, with the long vowel occupying two mātrās or units of pronunciation.

3. A script must support rare grammatical forms

A letter does not need to begin many words to be legitimate. ೠ is needed as an independent vowel whenever ṝ occurs without a preceding consonant, such as when:

naming or discussing the vowel itself
reciting the alphabet
writing grammatical explanations
representing rare textual forms
digitally encoding Sanskrit written in Kannada script

Its much more useful partner is the vowel sign ೄ, because long ṝ normally occurs after a consonant in inflected words.

4. The ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ is partly a phonological framework

The traditional alphabet is not merely a list of frequently used letters. It is also a systematic classification of possible and grammatically recognized sounds. Consequently, rare sounds may remain in the alphabet for completeness.

One important distinction

Some contemporary Kannada alphabet charts omit ೠ, while more complete or Sanskrit-inclusive charts retain it. This produces two slightly different ideas of the “Kannada alphabet”:

Practical modern Kannada alphabet: letters regularly needed for Kannada
Traditional Sanskrit-compatible Kannada script inventory: includes ೠ, ಌ, ೡ and their corresponding signs

The rare vowels are therefore better understood as Sanskrit-supporting characters in the Kannada script, rather than as productive vowels of the Kannada language.

Bottom line

ೠ is the independent Kannada-script letter for Sanskrit long vocalic ṝ. No common Kannada word begins with it, and normal Sanskrit words almost never begin with it either. It remains in the ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ because Kannada script traditionally supports Sanskrit, and ṝ is required in Sanskrit grammar and inflection, usually through its vowel sign ೄ.

A good analogy is that ೠ is a complete letter with an extremely specialized job. Its independent form is almost never needed, but its dependent sign ೄ is occasionally necessary when accurately writing Sanskrit grammatical forms.

You said: I need more examples of ೠ form used in Kannada and Sanskrit of/with any consonant.

Copilot said: Yes. The key distinction is between ೠ, the independent vowel, and ೄ, the dependent vowel sign attached to a consonant. In genuine Sanskrit forms, ೄ is much more common than standalone ೠ.

1. ೠ versus ೄ
Form	Name	Usageೠ	Independent long vocalic ṝ	Used when the syllable begins directly with ṝ
ೄ	Dependent long vocalic ṝ sign	Added to a consonant, as in ತೄ = tṝ

Compare:

ಋ = ṛ
ೠ = ṝ
ಕೃ = kṛ
ಕೄ = kṝ

In Sanskrit grammar, ṝ is the long counterpart of ṛ. It occurs mainly in particular inflected forms of nouns ending in-ṛ, especially kinship terms and agent nouns.

2. Genuine Sanskrit examples written in Kannada script

These are Sanskrit forms represented in Kannada script, not ordinarily native Kannada words.

ಪಿತೃ, father or ancestor
Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningಪಿತೄನ್	पितॄन्	pitṝn	fathers/ancestors, accusative plural
ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್	पितॄणाम्	pitṝṇām	of the fathers/ancestors

A genuine Vedic example is पितॄन्, pitṝn, occurring in the Ṛgveda. In Kannada script, that form is ಪಿತೄನ್.

Thus:

ಪಿ + ತ್ + ೄ + ನ್ = ಪಿತೄನ್

Here the long-ṝ sign ೄ is attached to ತ:

ತ + ೄ = ತೄ

ಮಾತೃ, mother
Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningಮಾತೄಃ	मातॄः	mātṝḥ	mothers, accusative plural
ಮಾತೄಣಾಮ್	मातॄणाम्	mātṝṇām	of the mothers

Formation:

ಮಾತೃ = mātṛ, mother
ಮಾತೄಃ = mātṝḥ, mothers
ಮಾತೄಣಾಮ್ = mātṝṇām, of the mothers

Again, the actual combination is:

ತ + ೄ = ತೄ

ಭ್ರಾತೃ, brother
Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningಭ್ರಾತೄನ್	भ्रातॄन्	bhrātṝn	brothers, accusative plural
ಭ್ರಾತೄಣಾಮ್	भ्रातॄणाम्	bhrātṝṇām	of the brothers

Formation:

ಭ್ರಾತೃ = bhrātṛ, brother
ಭ್ರಾತೄನ್ = bhrātṝn, brothers
ಭ್ರಾತೄಣಾಮ್ = bhrātṝṇām, of the brothers
ದುಹಿತೃ, daughter
Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningದುಹಿತೄಃ	दुहितॄः	duhitṝḥ	daughters, accusative plural
ದುಹಿತೄಣಾಮ್	दुहितॄणाम्	duhitṝṇām	of the daughters

Here too:

ದುಹಿತೃ → ದುಹಿತೄಣಾಮ್

ಕರ್ತೃ, doer or agent
Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningಕರ್ತೄನ್	कर्तॄन्	kartṝn	doers, accusative plural
ಕರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್	कर्तॄणाम्	kartṝṇām	of the doers

Formation:

ಕರ್ತೃ = kartṛ, doer
ಕರ್ತೄನ್ = kartṝn, doers
ಕರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್ = kartṝṇām, of the doers

Notice that the sign still attaches to ತ, although the full cluster is ರ್ತೄ:

ರ್ + ತ + ೄ = ರ್ತೄ

3. More agent-noun examples

Sanskrit has many agent nouns ending in-ತೃ, -tṛ. In certain plural forms, the final ತೃ becomes ತೄ.

Basic form	Long-ṝ form	IAST	Meaningದಾತೃ	ದಾತೄಣಾಮ್	dātṝṇām	of the givers
ನೇತೃ	ನೇತೄಣಾಮ್	netṝṇām	of the leaders
ಭರ್ತೃ	ಭರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್	bhartṝṇām	of the maintainers, husbands
ಹರ್ತೃ	ಹರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್	hartṝṇām	of the takers or removers
ವಕ್ತೃ	ವಕ್ತೄಣಾಮ್	vaktṝṇām	of the speakers
ಶ್ರೋತೃ	ಶ್ರೋತೄಣಾಮ್	śrotṝṇām	of the listeners
ದ್ರಷ್ಟೃ	ದ್ರಷ್ಟೄಣಾಮ್	draṣṭṝṇām	of the observers or seers
ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ	ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೄಣಾಮ್	sraṣṭṝṇām	of the creators
ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ	ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣಾಮ್	rakṣitṝṇām	of the protectors
ಜನಯಿತೃ	ಜನಯಿತೄಣಾಮ್	janayitṝṇām	of the progenitors
ಪ್ರದಾತೃ	ಪ್ರದಾತೄಣಾಮ್	pradātṝṇām	of the donors
ವಿಧಾತೃ	ವಿಧಾತೄಣಾಮ್	vidhātṝṇām	of the ordainers or creators

The grammatical pattern is:

-ತೃ → -ತೄನ್ / -ತೄಃ / -ತೄಣಾಮ್

The precise ending depends on the word’s gender and grammatical case.

4. Examples where ೄ attaches to consonants other than ತ

Most familiar examples contain ತೄ, because numerous Sanskrit kinship and agent nouns end in-ತೃ. But long ṝ can occur after other consonants too.

ನೃ, man or person

The Sanskrit word ನೃ, nṛ means “man,” “person,” or sometimes “hero.”

Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningನೄನ್	नॄन्	nṝn	men, accusative plural
ನೄಣಾಮ್	नॄणाम्	nṝṇām	of the men

Here:

ನ + ೄ = ನೄ

This is an especially useful example because it proves that ೄ is not inherently part of ತೄ. It is an independent vowel sign capable of joining another consonant.

ಸ್ವಸೃ, sister

The Sanskrit word ಸ್ವಸೃ, svasṛ means “sister.”

Kannada script	Devanagari	IAST	Meaningಸ್ವಸೄಃ	स्वसॄः	svasṝḥ	sisters, accusative plural
ಸ್ವಸೄಣಾಮ್	स्वसॄणाम्	svasṝṇām	of the sisters

Here:

ಸ + ೄ = ಸೄ

Therefore, we have at least three consonant combinations found in real Sanskrit morphology:

ತೄ = tṝ
ನೄ = nṝ
ಸೄ = sṝ
5. A real textual example

One form actually found in the Ṛgveda is:

Devanagari

पितॄन्

Kannada script

ಪಿತೄನ್

IAST

pitṝn

Meaning

“the ancestors” or “the fathers,” in the accusative plural.

The form appears, for example, in Ṛgveda 10.15.3, in the expression पितॄन्, pitṝn.

The important visual portion is:

तॄ = ತೄ

So this is not merely a theoretically possible character. It occurs in genuine Sanskrit textual and grammatical usage.

6. Can ೄ be placed on every Kannada consonant?

Technically, yes. As a script operation, the vowel sign ೄ can be attached to Kannada consonant characters.

ಕ-varga

ಕೄ ಖೄ ಗೄ ಘೄ ಙೄ

Transliteration:

kṝ khṝ gṝ ghṝ ṅṝ

ಚ-varga

ಚೄ ಛೄ ಜೄ ಝೄ ಞೄ

Transliteration:

cṝ chṝ jṝ jhṝ ñṝ

ಟ-varga

ಟೄ ಠೄ ಡೄ ಢೄ ಣೄ

Transliteration:

ṭṝ ṭhṝ ḍṝ ḍhṝ ṇṝ

ತ-varga

ತೄ ಥೄ ದೄ ಧೄ ನೄ

Transliteration:

tṝ thṝ dṝ dhṝ nṝ

ಪ-varga

ಪೄ ಫೄ ಬೄ ಭೄ ಮೄ

Transliteration:

pṝ phṝ bṝ bhṝ mṝ

Other consonants

ಯೄ ರೄ ಲೄ ವೄ ಶೄ ಷೄ ಸೄ ಹೄ ಳೄ

Transliteration:

yṝ rṝ lṝ vṝ śṝ ṣṝ sṝ hṝ ḷṝ

However, this requires an important qualification:

These combinations are orthographically possible syllables, but most are not found in actual Kannada or Sanskrit words.

For example, ಕೄ is technically writable and pronounced approximately kṝ, but that does not mean there is a commonly attested word containing it.

7. Is ೄ used in native Kannada words?

Practically, no.

Modern Kannada normally keeps the short ೃ in Sanskrit-derived stems:

ಪಿತೃ
ಮಾತೃ
ಭ್ರಾತೃ
ಕರ್ತೃ
ನೇತೃ
ದಾತೃ

When Kannada adds its own grammatical endings, it normally does so according to Kannada morphology:

ಪಿತೃಗಳು = fathers
ಪಿತೃಗಳ = of the fathers
ಮಾತೃಗಳು = mothers
ಮಾತೃಗಳ = of the mothers
ಕರ್ತೃಗಳು = doers
ಕರ್ತೃಗಳ = of the doers
ದಾತೃಗಳು = donors
ದಾತೃಗಳ = of the donors

Kannada does not normally convert these into:

ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್
ಮಾತೄಣಾಮ್
ಕರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್

Those are Sanskrit grammatical forms written in Kannada script.

Therefore:

ಪಿತೃಗಳ is Kannada.
ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್ is Sanskrit written in Kannada letters.

That distinction is central.

8. Best examples to remember

If you want a concise teaching list, these are the clearest examples:

ಪಿತೄನ್ — pitṝn, the fathers/ancestors
ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್ — pitṝṇām, of the fathers
ಮಾತೄಣಾಮ್ — mātṝṇām, of the mothers
ಭ್ರಾತೄಣಾಮ್ — bhrātṝṇām, of the brothers
ದುಹಿತೄಣಾಮ್ — duhitṝṇām, of the daughters
ಕರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್ — kartṝṇām, of the doers
ದಾತೄಣಾಮ್ — dātṝṇām, of the givers
ನೇತೄಣಾಮ್ — netṝṇām, of the leaders
ನೄಣಾಮ್ — nṝṇām, of the men
ಸ್ವಸೄಣಾಮ್ — svasṝṇām, of the sisters
Final takeaway

ೠ is rarely used independently. Its attached form ೄ occurs genuinely in Sanskrit, primarily in inflections of words ending in ಋ/ೃ, especially:

ತೃ → ತೄ
ನೃ → ನೄ
ಸೃ → ಸೄ

In modern Kannada writing, these forms appear mainly when reproducing Sanskrit accurately. They are not productive forms of ordinary Kannada grammar.

You said: are you saying we do no sandhi in Kannada with ಋ that will lead to ೠ?

Copilot said: Yes, sandhi can lead from ಋ to ೠ/ೄ. My earlier answer was too categorical. I should have distinguished native Kannada sandhi from Sanskrit savarṇa-dīrgha sandhi as used in Sanskrit words written in Kannada script.

The relevant rule

In Sanskrit ಸವರ್ಣದೀರ್ಘ ಸಂಧಿ, vowels of the same class combine into their corresponding long vowel:

ಅ/ಆ + ಅ/ಆ → ಆ
ಇ/ಈ + ಇ/ಈ → ಈ
ಉ/ಊ + ಉ/ಊ → ಊ
ಋ/ೠ + ಋ/ೠ → ೠ

This follows ಪಾಣಿನಿ’s rule:

ಅಕಃ ಸವರ್ಣೇ ದೀರ್ಘಃ
 akaḥ savarṇe dīrghaḥ

The vowel classes include ಋ and ೠ as short and long members of the same class. Therefore, two adjacent vocalic-r vowels coalesce into long ṝ.

In Kannada script

When the resulting long vowel follows a consonant, we use the vowel sign ೄ, not the independent letter ೠ.

For example:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ = ಪಿತೄಣ

Breaking it down:

ಪಿತೃ ends in ೃ, that is ṛ
ಋಣ begins with ಋ, that is ṛ
ṛ + ṛ → ṝ
ತ + ೄ → ತೄ
Result: ಪಿತೄಣ, pitṝṇa

This means “ancestral debt” or “debt owed to one’s ancestors.”

Likewise, at least as a regular Sanskrit sandhi derivation:

ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ = ಮಾತೄಣ
 mātṛ + ṛṇa → mātṝṇa
 “debt owed to one’s mother”

Important distinction: ೠ versus ೄ

The sandhi result is phonologically ೠ, ṝ, but its written form depends on its position:

If it follows a consonant

Use the dependent sign ೄ:

ತ್ + ೠ sound → ತೄ
ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
If it begins a syllable independently

Use ೠ itself.

Therefore, saying “ಋ + ಋ becomes ೠ” describes the vowel result. Inside a written word, that result will normally appear as ೄ attached to the preceding consonant.

Why is this rarely visible in Kannada?

There are three reasons.

1. It is primarily Sanskrit sandhi

Kannada grammar books teach ಸವರ್ಣದೀರ್ಘ ಸಂಧಿ under ಸಂಸ್ಕೃತ ಸಂಧಿಗಳು, because forms such as:

ಕವಿ + ಇಂದ್ರ → ಕವೀಂದ್ರ
ಗುರು + ಉಪದೇಶ → ಗುರೂಪದೇಶ
ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ

follow Sanskrit phonological rules. Kannada instructional sources commonly describe savarṇa-dīrgha as occurring when Sanskrit words meet, although elementary presentations often list only ಅ, ಇ and ಉ examples and omit the much rarer ಋ case.

2. The combination ಋ + ಋ is extremely uncommon

Many words end in Sanskrit-ಋ/-ೃ:

ಪಿತೃ
ಮಾತೃ
ಭ್ರಾತೃ
ಕರ್ತೃ
ದಾತೃ

But very few commonly combined following words begin with ಋ:

ಋಣ
ಋಷಿ
ಋತು
ಋಕ್

Therefore, the environment needed for this sandhi occurs only rarely.

3. Modern Kannada often retains transparent component forms

In contemporary Kannada, writers may prefer:

ಪಿತೃಋಣ
ಪಿತೃ ಋಣ
sometimes the familiarized spelling ಪಿತೃಋಣ

instead of the strictly Sanskrit-sandhied:

ಪಿತೄಣ

This is because ಪಿತೃಋಣ makes the two components immediately recognizable to a modern Kannada reader. The unsandhied form may therefore be more common in ordinary Kannada prose, even though ಪಿತೄಣ follows the classical Sanskrit sandhi rule.

Native Kannada sandhi versus Sanskrit sandhi

This is the important correction.

Native Kannada word formation

Kannada does not have productive native stems ending in the vocalic ಋ or native words normally beginning with it. Consequently, a productive native-Kannada rule generating ೠ is virtually absent.

You do not ordinarily encounter native combinations comparable to:

native Kannada word ending in ಋ + native Kannada word beginning in ಋ

The required native vocabulary is essentially unavailable.

Sanskrit-derived compounds in Kannada

When Sanskrit words are compounded according to Sanskrit grammar and written in Kannada script, the rule is valid:

ಋ + ಋ = ೠ

or, after a consonant:

ೃ + ಋ = ೄ

Thus Kannada script can absolutely display ೠ/ೄ as the result of sandhi, but the process belongs primarily to the Sanskrit grammatical layer used within literary or Sanskritized Kannada.

Another necessary correction to my previous examples

Forms such as:

ಪಿತೄನ್, pitṝn
ಪಿತೄಣಾಮ್, pitṝṇām
ಕರ್ತೄಣಾಮ್, kartṝṇām

do contain ೄ, but they are not sandhi examples. They are Sanskrit declensional or inflectional forms.

By contrast:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ

is a true sandhi example because two separately occurring vowels meet at a word boundary.

Precise conclusion

Yes, ಋ can become ೠ through ಸವರ್ಣದೀರ್ಘ ಸಂಧಿ: ಋ + ಋ = ೠ. When this occurs after a consonant, the written result is the dependent sign ೄ, as in ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ.

However:

this is mainly a Sanskrit sandhi rule
it can be represented fully in Kannada script
it is extremely rare in actual Kannada usage
modern Kannada may preserve the visibly separated form ಪಿತೃಋಣ
the existence of this sandhi possibility is indeed one legitimate reason that ೠ and ೄ belong in the Sanskrit-compatible Kannada ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ

So your intuition was correct: sandhi is another route by which ೠ/ೄ can arise, not only Sanskrit noun inflection.

You said: Give me more sandhis that are natural. Pitru Runa is natural and used in Kannada. Give me more sandhis with different words for each parts. Give me like 100.

Copilot said: You are asking for natural, meaningful compounds actually usable in Kannada, not mechanically manufactured combinations. Under that standard, I should not pretend there are 100 good examples. The phonological rule can generate countless forms, but natural lexical examples are severely limited because the first word must end in ಋ/ೃ and the second must begin with ಋ.

First, the rule

In Sanskrit savarṇa-dīrgha sandhi:

ಋ/ೠ + ಋ/ೠ → ೠ

When the first ಋ is attached to a consonant as ೃ, the resulting long vowel is written with ೄ:

ತೃ + ಋ… → ತೄ…

Thus:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
 pitṛ + ṛṇa → pitṝṇa

The combined form contains the vowel sign ೄ, while ೠ is its independent equivalent.

Why 100 natural examples are not realistically available

To produce this exact sandhi:

The first component must end in vocalic ಋ, such as:

ಪಿತೃ
ಮಾತೃ
ಭ್ರಾತೃ
ಕರ್ತೃ
ದಾತೃ
ನೇತೃ
ವಕ್ತೃ

The second component must begin with vocalic ಋ, such as:

ಋಣ
ಋಷಿ
ಋತು
ಋತ
ಋಕ್
ಋಜು
ಋದ್ಧಿ

The resulting compound must make semantic sense and preferably have actual conventional usage.

Although Sanskrit has many-ತೃ agent nouns, the number of familiar words beginning with ಋ is small. Consequently, 100 mechanically possible combinations could be generated, but most would be artificial formations, not natural Kannada or established Sanskrit compounds. Sanskrit’s compounding system is highly productive, but grammatical possibility does not establish conventional usage or textual attestation.

The strongest natural examples
1. ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to one’s father or ancestors

This is by far the strongest example because ಪಿತೃಋಣ is culturally familiar in Kannada. The sandhied Sanskrit form is:

ಪಿತೄಣ

In ordinary Kannada, however, the transparent spelling ಪಿತೃಋಣ is more readily understood and often preferred.

2. ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಮಾತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to one’s mother

ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಮಾತೄಣ
 mātṛ + ṛṇa → mātṝṇa

This is semantically natural, particularly in ethical, religious and literary writing. Kannada speakers are more likely to write ಮಾತೃಋಣ, but ಮಾತೄಣ is the classical sandhied formation.

3. ಗುರು is not an example

It is useful to eliminate a likely misconception:

ಗುರು + ಋಣ ≠ ಗುರೄಣ

The final vowel of ಗುರು is ಉ, not ಋ. Therefore, it does not produce ೠ.

Depending on grammatical treatment and compounding convention, ಗುರುಋಣ would not be an example of ṛ + ṛ savarṇa-dīrgha.

Natural or defensible formations

The following are grammatically meaningful formations. However, they do not all have the same degree of established Kannada usage.

With ಋಣ, debt or obligation
4. ಭ್ರಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಭ್ರಾತೄಣ

Meaning: debt or obligation owed to a brother

ಭ್ರಾತೄಣ
 bhrātṝṇa

The idea is understandable, but the combined form is uncommon in Kannada. One would normally say ಸಹೋದರನ ಋಣ or retain ಭ್ರಾತೃಋಣ in Sanskritized writing.

5. ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಕರ್ತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to the maker, author or creator

ಕರ್ತೄಣ
 kartṝṇa

This is semantically possible in a philosophical or literary context, but it is not a familiar Kannada lexical item.

6. ದಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ದಾತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to a donor or benefactor

ದಾತೄಣ
 dātṝṇa

This is a particularly reasonable coined compound because the concept of gratitude or obligation to a donor is natural.

7. ನೇತೃ + ಋಣ → ನೇತೄಣ

Meaning: debt or obligation owed to a leader

ನೇತೄಣ
 netṝṇa

Grammatically possible, but Kannada would ordinarily use ನಾಯಕನ ಋಣ.

8. ಶಿಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಶಿಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to an instructor

ಶಿಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ
 śikṣitṝṇa

This is structurally possible but much less natural than ಗುರುಋಣ or ಗುರುವಿನ ಋಣ.

9. ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to a protector

ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ
 rakṣitṝṇa

This makes semantic sense, but it is best considered a transparent Sanskrit formation rather than an established Kannada word.

10. ಜನಯಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಜನಯಿತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to a progenitor or parent

ಜನಯಿತೄಣ
 janayitṝṇa

This overlaps considerably with ಪಿತೄಣ and ಮಾತೄಣ.

11. ಭರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಭರ್ತೄಣ

Meaning: debt owed to a supporter, maintainer or husband

ಭರ್ತೄಣ
 bhartṝṇa

Possible, but context is essential because ಭರ್ತೃ has multiple meanings.

12. ನೇತೃ + ಋಣ → ನೇತೄಣ

Meaning: obligation toward the person who guided or led someone

This is meaningful but not an established everyday compound.

With ಋಷಿ, sage

These are grammatically constructible, but ಋಷಿ beginning as the second component rarely produces conventional compounds with agent nouns as the first component.

13. ಪಿತೃ + ಋಷಿ → ಪಿತೄಷಿ

pitṛ + ṛṣi → pitṝṣi

Possible intended meaning: an ancestral sage or “a sage among the ancestors.”

However, this is not the normal established way to express that idea. Sanskrit would more naturally use words related to ರಾಜರ್ಷಿ, ದೇವರ್ಷಿ, ಬ್ರಹ್ಮರ್ಷಿ or a syntactically clearer phrase.

14. ಮಾತೃ + ಋಷಿ → ಮಾತೄಷಿ

mātṛ + ṛṣi → mātṝṣi

Possible literal interpretation: “maternal sage” or “mother-sage.”

The form is grammatically derivable but not naturally lexicalized.

15. ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಷಿ → ಕರ್ತೄಷಿ

kartṛ + ṛṣi → kartṝṣi

Possible interpretation: “a sage who is a creator/doer.”

Again, this is not an established everyday compound.

These examples show why merely applying the rule does not produce natural vocabulary.

With ಋತು, season
16. ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತು → ಕರ್ತೄತು

kartṛ + ṛtu → kartṝtu

Although the sandhi operation is predictable, the compound has no obvious natural meaning. This is exactly the kind of formally possible but lexically unnatural result that should not be listed as a genuine example.

17. ಪಿತೃ + ಋತು → ಪಿತೄತು

pitṛ + ṛtu → pitṝtu

One could invent a meaning such as “the season of the ancestors,” but the established expression would be ಪಿತೃಪಕ್ಷ, not ಪಿತೄತು.

Therefore, this should not be presented as a natural word.

With ಋಕ್, a Vedic verse
18. ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಕ್ → ಕರ್ತೄಕ್

Possible intended meaning: “a verse concerning the agent” or “a verse composed by an author.”

But this is not a natural lexical compound. Moreover, the final form can be affected by the morphology and phonological treatment of ಋಕ್, so it should not be casually offered as a clean modern-Kannada example.

19. ಪಿತೃ + ಋಕ್ → ಪಿತೄಕ್

Possible intended meaning: “a verse to the ancestors.”

An actually meaningful Sanskrit expression would more likely use:

ಪಿತೃಸೂಕ್ತ
ಪಿತೃಋಕ್, if retaining the components analytically
a phrase meaning “verse addressed to the ancestors”

The mechanically sandhied form is not necessarily the conventional lexical choice.

A carefully graded list

Here is the honest classification.

Category A: Natural and culturally intelligible
ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಮಾತೄಣ

These are the strongest examples.

Category B: Semantically natural but uncommon as fixed words
ಭ್ರಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಭ್ರಾತೄಣ
ದಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ದಾತೄಣ
ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಕರ್ತೄಣ
ನೇತೃ + ಋಣ → ನೇತೄಣ
ಭರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಭರ್ತೄಣ
ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ
ಶಿಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಶಿಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ
ಜನಯಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಜನಯಿತೄಣ
ಪೋಷಯಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪೋಷಯಿತೄಣ
ಪ್ರದಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪ್ರದಾತೄಣ
ಉಪಕಾರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಉಪಕಾರ್ತೄಣ
ನಿರ್ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ನಿರ್ಮಾತೄಣ
ಸಂರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಸಂರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ

These can be understood, but most are not established Kannada headwords.

Category C: Mechanically derivable but not natural enough to recommend

Examples such as:

ಪಿತೄಷಿ
ಮಾತೄಷಿ
ಕರ್ತೄಷಿ
ಪಿತೄತು
ಮಾತೄತು
ಕರ್ತೄತು
ಪಿತೄಕ್
ಕರ್ತೄಕ್

I would not count these as good natural examples.

Why even the natural forms are rarely written with ೄ

There is a deeper question here: if the sandhi is valid, why do we more often see ಪಿತೃಋಣ than ಪಿತೄಣ?

Kannada readers preserve the compound boundary

The spelling:

ಪಿತೃಋಣ

makes both words immediately visible:

ಪಿತೃ
ಋಣ

The spelling:

ಪಿತೄಣ

is phonologically compressed and can be harder for modern readers to analyze.

Kannada does not consistently reproduce Sanskrit external sandhi

A Sanskrit-derived compound may enter Kannada in:

a fully sandhied form,
a partially Sanskritized form,
an unsandhied transparent form, or
a Kannada syntactic form.

Therefore, several expressions may coexist:

ಪಿತೄಣ
ಪಿತೃಋಣ
ಪಿತೃ ಋಣ
ಪಿತೃಗಳ ಋಣ
ಪೂರ್ವಜರ ಋಣ

They are not necessarily identical in grammatical register.

Orthographic sandhi and spoken Kannada are different

A speaker may pronounce ಪಿತೃಋಣ approximately as pitru-runa without producing a carefully sustained Sanskrit long vocalic ṝ. Thus, the classical spelling ಪಿತೄಣ may not accurately represent contemporary Kannada pronunciation.

Why I should not give you 100

I can easily generate a grid containing:

25 first components ending in-ತೃ
4 second components beginning in ಋ
100 sandhied outputs

But that would not satisfy your requirement of natural usage. It would produce items such as “listener-season,” “speaker-sage,” “protector-verse,” and “creator-straightness,” many of which would be semantically arbitrary.

A dictionary or corpus should be used to distinguish attested words from mechanically generated forms. The Digital Corpus of Sanskrit provides sandhi-split, morphologically tagged attestations from Sanskrit texts, while the Sanskrit Heritage Site provides dictionary, parsing, morphology and phonological tools. Those resources are appropriate for testing whether a proposed form is actually attested rather than merely grammatical.

Corrected conclusion

ಪಿತೄಣ is a valid, natural, culturally meaningful example. ಮಾತೄಣ is also quite defensible. Beyond those, combinations with ಋಣ can be created meaningfully, but the further we proceed, the more they become productive Sanskrit formations rather than established Kannada words.

So the accurate answer is:

2 very strong examples
approximately 10 to 15 semantically defensible formations
not 100 separately natural, established examples

Giving you 100 without marking most of them as artificial would be misleading. The rarity of such examples actually explains why ೠ/ೄ exists in the alphabet but is almost never encountered in ordinary Kannada text.

You said: well I meant I need 50 words that end with ಋ or its symbol with consonant and 50 words that start with ಋ. These should meaning fully combine. Try as much you can.

Copilot said: Yes, that makes the request much clearer. You want 50 distinct words ending in ಋ/ೃ and 50 distinct expressions beginning with ಋ, paired so that each combination has a coherent meaning and produces ೄ through Sanskrit savarṇa-dīrgha sandhi.

Sanskrit has many agent nouns ending in-ತೃ, meaning “one who does,” comparable to English “-er.” Standard examples include ಕರ್ತೃ, ಗಂತೃ, ಹಂತೃ, alongside relational words such as ಪಿತೃ, ಮಾತೃ, ಭ್ರಾತೃ and ಸ್ವಸೃ. A lexical category lists more than seventy Sanskrit ṛ-stem nouns, so finding 50 valid first components is feasible.

The pattern

Xೃ + ಋY → XೄY

For example:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
 pitṛ + ṛṇa → pitṝṇa

The pairings below range from familiar expressions to grammatically regular, meaningful Sanskritic compounds suitable for formal Kannada. I mark their naturalness:

A: natural or immediately understandable
B: semantically natural, but uncommon as a closed compound
C: specialized or newly constructible; grammatical, but should be defined when first used
50 meaningful combinations
Family, teachers and social relationships

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
 pitṛ + ṛṇa → pitṝṇa
 Debt or obligation to one’s father or ancestors. A

ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣಭಾರ → ಮಾತೄಣಭಾರ
 mātṛ + ṛṇabhāra → mātṝṇabhāra
 The burden of one’s obligation to one’s mother. A/B

ಭ್ರಾತೃ + ಋಣಸಂಬಂಧ → ಭ್ರಾತೄಣಸಂಬಂಧ
 bhrātṛ + ṛṇasambandha → bhrātṝṇasambandha
 A relationship of obligation to a brother. B

ಸ್ವಸೃ + ಋಣಸ್ಮರಣೆ → ಸ್ವಸೄಣಸ್ಮರಣೆ
 svasṛ + ṛṇasmaraṇa → svasṝṇasmaraṇa
 Remembrance of one’s indebtedness to a sister. B

ದುಹಿತೃ + ಋಣಪ್ರಜ್ಞೆ → ದುಹಿತೄಣಪ್ರಜ್ಞೆ
 duhitṛ + ṛṇaprajñā → duhitṝṇaprajñā
 Awareness of one’s obligation toward a daughter. B

ಜಾಮಾತೃ + ಋಣಬಂಧ → ಜಾಮಾತೄಣಬಂಧ
 jāmātṛ + ṛṇabandha → jāmātṝṇabandha
 A bond of obligation involving a son-in-law. B/C

ಜನಿತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ವೀಕಾರ → ಜನಿತೄಣಸ್ವೀಕಾರ
 janitṛ + ṛṇasvīkāra → janitṝṇasvīkāra
 Acknowledgement of one’s debt to a progenitor or parent. B

ಜನಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಜ್ಞಾನ → ಜನಯಿತೄಣಜ್ಞಾನ
 janayitṛ + ṛṇajñāna → janayitṝṇajñāna
 Knowledge or recognition of one’s obligation to a parent or progenitor. B

ಪೋಷಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಭಾವ → ಪೋಷಯಿತೄಣಭಾವ
 poṣayitṛ + ṛṇabhāva → poṣayitṝṇabhāva
 A sense of indebtedness to the person who nurtured or supported one. A/B

ಶಿಕ್ಷಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಕೃತಜ್ಞತೆ → ಶಿಕ್ಷಯಿತೄಣಕೃತಜ್ಞತೆ
 śikṣayitṛ + ṛṇakṛtajñatā → śikṣayitṝṇakṛtajñatā
 Gratitude arising from one’s debt to a teacher. B

Givers, supporters and protectors

ದಾತೃ + ಋಣಬೋಧ → ದಾತೄಣಬೋಧ
 dātṛ + ṛṇabodha → dātṝṇabodha
 Awareness of one’s debt to a donor. A/B

ಪ್ರದಾತೃ + ಋಣಪಾವತಿ → ಪ್ರದಾತೄಣಪಾವತಿ
 pradātṛ + ṛṇapāvati → pradātṝṇapāvati
 Repayment of an obligation to a provider or donor. B

ಅನ್ನದಾತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ಮೃತಿ → ಅನ್ನದಾತೄಣಸ್ಮೃತಿ
 annadātṛ + ṛṇasmṛti → annadātṝṇasmṛti
 Remembrance of one’s debt to the provider of food. A/B

ಜ್ಞಾನದಾತೃ + ಋಣಗೌರವ → ಜ್ಞಾನದಾತೄಣಗೌರವ
 jñānadātṛ + ṛṇagaurava → jñānadātṝṇagaurava
 Respect arising from indebtedness to the giver of knowledge. B

ಆಶ್ರಯದಾತೃ + ಋಣಪರಿಹಾರ → ಆಶ್ರಯದಾತೄಣಪರಿಹಾರ
 āśrayadātṛ + ṛṇaparihāra → āśrayadātṝṇaparihāra
 Discharge of one’s obligation to a person who provided shelter. B

ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣಶೋಧನೆ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣಶೋಧನೆ
 rakṣitṛ + ṛṇaśodhana → rakṣitṝṇaśodhana
 Repayment of one’s debt to a protector. B

ತ್ರಾತೃ + ಋಣನಿರ್ವಹಣೆ → ತ್ರಾತೄಣನಿರ್ವಹಣೆ
 trātṛ + ṛṇanirvahaṇa → trātṝṇanirvahaṇa
 Fulfilment of an obligation to a rescuer or saviour. B

ಗೋಪ್ತೃ + ಋಣಪ್ರತಿದಾನ → ಗೋಪ್ತೄಣಪ್ರತಿದಾನ
 goptṛ + ṛṇapratidāna → goptṝṇapratidāna
 Recompense offered to a guardian or protector. B

ಧರ್ತೃ + ಋಣಮೋಚನೆ → ಧರ್ತೄಣಮೋಚನೆ
 dhartṛ + ṛṇamocana → dhartṝṇamocana
 Release from an obligation to the person who sustained or upheld something. B/C

ಭರ್ತೃ + ಋಣನಿವೃತ್ತಿ → ಭರ್ತೄಣನಿವೃತ್ತಿ
 bhartṛ + ṛṇanivṛtti → bhartṝṇanivṛtti
 Discharge of an obligation to a supporter, maintainer or husband. B

Makers, authors and knowledge workers

ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಣನಿರ್ಣಯ → ಕರ್ತೄಣನಿರ್ಣಯ
 kartṛ + ṛṇanirṇaya → kartṝṇanirṇaya
 Determination of an obligation owed to the maker or responsible agent. B

ನಿರ್ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣಮೌಲ್ಯ → ನಿರ್ಮಾತೄಣಮೌಲ್ಯ
 nirmātṛ + ṛṇamūlya → nirmātṝṇamūlya
 The value assigned to an obligation toward a creator or builder. B/C

ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ + ಋಣಚಿಂತನೆ → ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೄಣಚಿಂತನೆ
 sraṣṭṛ + ṛṇacintana → sraṣṭṝṇacintana
 Reflection on one’s debt to a creator. B

ರಚಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ವೀಕೃತಿ → ರಚಯಿತೄಣಸ್ವೀಕೃತಿ
 racayitṛ + ṛṇasvīkṛti → racayitṝṇasvīkṛti
 Acknowledgement of intellectual or artistic debt to an author. A/B

ಲೇಖಿತೃ + ಋಣಲೇಖನ → ಲೇಖಿತೄಣಲೇಖನ
 lekhitṛ + ṛṇalēkhana → lekhitṝṇalēkhana
 Written acknowledgement of a debt to a writer. B/C

ವಕ್ತೃ + ಋಣೋಕ್ತಿ → ವಕ್ತೄಣೋಕ್ತಿ
 vaktṛ + ṛṇokti → vaktṝṇokti
 A statement acknowledging indebtedness to a speaker. B/C

ಆಖ್ಯಾತೃ + ಋಣನಿರೂಪಣೆ → ಆಖ್ಯಾತೄಣನಿರೂಪಣೆ
 ākhyātṛ + ṛṇanirūpaṇa → ākhyātṝṇanirūpaṇa
 Explanation of one’s debt to a narrator or exponent. C

ಜ್ಞಾತೃ + ಋಣಪರಿಜ್ಞಾನ → ಜ್ಞಾತೄಣಪರಿಜ್ಞಾನ
 jñātṛ + ṛṇaparijñāna → jñātṝṇaparijñāna
 Full recognition of an obligation to a knower or informant. C

ವೇತ್ತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ವೀಕೃತಿ → ವೇತ್ತೄಣಸ್ವೀಕೃತಿ
 vettṛ + ṛṇasvīkṛti → vettṝṇasvīkṛti
 Acknowledgement of intellectual debt to an expert. B

ಶ್ರೋತೃ + ಋಣಗೌರವ → ಶ್ರೋತೄಣಗೌರವ
 śrotṛ + ṛṇagaurava → śrotṝṇagaurava
 Respectful recognition of one’s obligation to a listener or audience. C

Sanskrit-ತೃ is a productive agent-forming suffix. Thus words such as “doer,” “speaker,” “listener,” “protector” and “giver” legitimately form ṛ-final stems, even when a particular compound is newly created for a specialized context.

Leaders, administrators and professionals

ನೇತೃ + ಋಣಾನುಬಂಧ → ನೇತೄಣಾನುಬಂಧ
 netṛ + ṛṇānubandha → netṝṇānubandha
 A continuing obligation toward a leader or guide. B

ಶಾಸ್ತೃ + ಋಣನಿಯಮ → ಶಾಸ್ತೄಣನಿಯಮ
 śāstṛ + ṛṇaniyama → śāstṝṇaniyama
 A rule concerning an obligation to a teacher, ruler or instructor. C

ಪ್ರಶಾಸ್ತೃ + ಋಣವ್ಯವಸ್ಥೆ → ಪ್ರಶಾಸ್ತೄಣವ್ಯವಸ್ಥೆ
 praśāstṛ + ṛṇavyavasthā → praśāstṝṇavyavasthā
 A system governing obligations toward an administrator. C

ನಿಯಂತೃ + ಋಣಜವಾಬ್ದಾರಿ → ನಿಯಂತೄಣಜವಾಬ್ದಾರಿ
 niyantṛ + ṛṇajavābdāri → niyantṝṇajavābdāri
 Responsibility arising from an obligation to a controller or regulator. C

ವಿಧಾತೃ + ಋಣಧರ್ಮ → ವಿಧಾತೄಣಧರ್ಮ
 vidhātṛ + ṛṇadharma → vidhātṝṇadharma
 A moral obligation to an ordainer, creator or lawgiver. B/C

ಪ್ರವರ್ತಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಪರಂಪರೆ → ಪ್ರವರ್ತಯಿತೄಣಪರಂಪರೆ
 pravartayitṛ + ṛṇaparamparā → pravartayitṝṇaparamparā
 A tradition of acknowledging debt to the founder of a movement. B

ಸಂಸ್ಥಾಪಯಿತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ಮಾರಕ → ಸಂಸ್ಥಾಪಯಿತೄಣಸ್ಮಾರಕ
 saṃsthāpayitṛ + ṛṇasmāraka → saṃsthāpayitṝṇasmāraka
 A memorial recognizing indebtedness to a founder. B

ಅಭಿಯಂತೃ + ಋಣಸ್ವೀಕರಣ → ಅಭಿಯಂತೄಣಸ್ವೀಕರಣ
 abhiyantṛ + ṛṇasvīkaraṇa → abhiyantṝṇasvīkaraṇa
 Recognition of technical or professional debt to an engineer. C

ಯೋಜಯಿತೃ + ಋಣದಾಖಲೆ → ಯೋಜಯಿತೄಣದಾಖಲೆ
 yojayitṛ + ṛṇadākhale → yojayitṝṇadākhale
 A record of obligation to a planner or organizer. C

ಸಂಚಾಲಯಿತೃ + ಋಣವಿವರಣೆ → ಸಂಚಾಲಯಿತೄಣವಿವರಣೆ
 sañcālayitṛ + ṛṇavivaraṇa → sañcālayitṝṇavivaraṇa
 An account of indebtedness to an operator or director. C

Performers and people carrying out actions

ಗಂತೃ + ಋಣಯಾತ್ರೆ → ಗಂತೄಣಯಾತ್ರೆ
 gantṛ + ṛṇayātrā → gantṝṇayātrā
 A journey undertaken by a traveller to fulfil an obligation. C

ಯಾತೃ + ಋಣಪೂರಣ → ಯಾತೄಣಪೂರಣ
 yātṛ + ṛṇapūraṇa → yātṝṇapūraṇa
 Fulfilment of an obligation owed to a traveller or pilgrim. C

ವೋಢೃ + ಋಣಭಾರ → ವೋಢೄಣಭಾರ
 voḍhṛ + ṛṇabhāra → voḍhṝṇabhāra
 The burden of obligation toward a bearer or carrier. C

ಕ್ರೇತೃ + ಋಣಖಾತೆ → ಕ್ರೇತೄಣಖಾತೆ
 kretṛ + ṛṇakhāta → kretṝṇakhāta
 A buyer’s credit or debt account. B/C

ವಿಕ್ರೇತೃ + ಋಣವಿವಾದ → ವಿಕ್ರೇತೄಣವಿವಾದ
 vikretṛ + ṛṇavivāda → vikretṝṇavivāda
 A dispute concerning a debt owed to a seller. B

ಭೋಕ್ತೃ + ಋಣವ್ಯವಹಾರ → ಭೋಕ್ತೄಣವ್ಯವಹಾರ
 bhoktṛ + ṛṇavyavahāra → bhoktṝṇavyavahāra
 A transaction involving the liability of a consumer or beneficiary. C

ಪಕ್ತೃ + ಋಣಪಾವತಿ → ಪಕ್ತೄಣಪಾವತಿ
 paktṛ + ṛṇapāvati → paktṝṇapāvati
 Repayment of an obligation to the person who prepared food. B/C

ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಣಸಂಕಲ್ಪ → ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೄಣಸಂಕಲ್ಪ
 yajñakartṛ + ṛṇasaṅkalpa → yajñakartṝṇasaṅkalpa
 A resolve to discharge an obligation associated with a ritual performer. C

ಹೋತೃ + ಋಣದಕ್ಷಿಣೆ → ಹೋತೄಣದಕ್ಷಿಣೆ
 hotṛ + ṛṇadakṣiṇā → hotṝṇadakṣiṇā
 An honorarium offered in discharge of an obligation to the invoking priest. B

ಉದ್ಗಾತೃ + ಋಣಪ್ರತಿಪೂರ್ತಿ → ಉದ್ಗಾತೄಣಪ್ರತಿಪೂರ್ತಿ
 udgātṛ + ṛṇapratipūrti → udgātṝṇapratipūrti
 Recompense fulfilling an obligation toward a Vedic chanter. B/C

Several of these first components, including ಕರ್ತೃ, ಕ್ರೇತೃ, ಗೋಪ್ತೃ, ಜನಿತೃ, ದಾತೃ, ನೇತೃ, ಭರ್ತೃ, ಭೋಕ್ತೃ, ವಕ್ತೃ, ವಿಕ್ರೇತೃ, ವಿಧಾತೃ, ಶ್ರೋತೃ, ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ, ಹಂತೃ and ಹೋತೃ, are listed as Sanskrit ṛ-stem nouns.

Strictly speaking, a few heads recur, such as ಋಣಭಾರ and ಋಣಪಾವತಿ, even though the full pairings and meanings differ. If every second component must also be lexically unique, they can be replaced with:

ಋಣಭಾರ → ಋಣಹೊಣೆ
second ಋಣಪಾವತಿ → ಋಣಪ್ರತಿಪಾವತಿ
second ಋಣಸ್ವೀಕೃತಿ → ಋಣಾಭಿಜ್ಞಾನ
second ಋಣಗೌರವ → ಋಣಾದರ
Important linguistic caution

Only a limited subset, especially ಪಿತೄಣ and ಮಾತೄಣ, has a realistic chance of occurring naturally as compact forms in Kannada discourse. Many of the others are productive Sanskritic compounds, meaning that they are grammatically and semantically constructible but not necessarily dictionary-established Kannada words.

In ordinary Kannada, people would generally prefer transparent expressions such as:

ದಾತೃಋಣ or ದಾತೃವಿನ ಋಣ
ರಕ್ಷಕನ ಋಣ
ಲೇಖಕನಿಗೆ ಇರುವ ಋಣ
ಗುರುವಿನ ಋಣ
ಪೋಷಕರ ಋಣ

rather than unfamiliar compressed forms such as ದಾತೄಣ or ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ.

The Kannada script can represent every resulting ṝ correctly, but grammatical producibility should not be confused with common lexical usage. Sanskrit dictionaries and morphological tools distinguish dictionary entries, inflected forms and productively generated compounds.

Best examples for demonstrating ೄ

If your purpose is to explain why ೠ/ೄ exists in the ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ, the cleanest set is:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಮಾತೄಣ
ದಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ದಾತೄಣ
ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಣ → ಕರ್ತೄಣ
ನೇತೃ + ಋಣ → ನೇತೄಣ
ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಣ
ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ + ಋಣ → ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೄಣ
ಜನಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಜನಿತೄಣ
ಪೋಷಯಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪೋಷಯಿತೄಣ
ಅನ್ನದಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಅನ್ನದಾತೄಣ

These clearly demonstrate the transformation:

ೃ + ಋ → ೄ

And that is the strongest sandhi-based justification for retaining ೠ and ೄ in the full Sanskrit-compatible Kannada alphabet.

You said: I want words that are fully different and not modification of word runa

Copilot said: You are right. You want the second members to be genuinely different lexical words beginning with ಋ, such as ಋಣ, ಋತು, ಋಷಿ, ಋಕ್, ಋತ, ಋಜು, not fifty extensions of ಋಣ.

That substantially narrows the list. Sanskrit has many words ending in-ತೃ, but relatively few independent lexical bases beginning with ಋ. Sanskrit dictionaries confirm that ಋ is a genuine initial vowel, but its initial lexical distribution is limited.

Valid sandhi pattern

ೃ + ಋ → ೄ

For example:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ

But a compound must satisfy three conditions:

First member genuinely ends in ಋ/ೃ
Second member genuinely begins with ಋ
The two meanings combine naturally

The third condition eliminates most mechanical pairings.

Independent words beginning with ಋ

Here is a defensible working inventory. These are not merely modifications of ಋಣ.

Kannada	IAST	General meaningಋಣ	ṛṇa	debt, obligation
ಋತ	ṛta	truth, cosmic order, right order
ಋತು	ṛtu	season, proper time
ಋಷಿ	ṛṣi	sage, seer
ಋಕ್	ṛc	Vedic verse or hymn
ಋಜು	ṛju	straight, upright, honest
ಋದ್ಧ	ṛddha	prosperous, increased, accomplished
ಋದ್ಧಿ	ṛddhi	prosperity, growth, accomplishment
ಋಕ್ಷ	ṛkṣa	bear; star or constellation in some contexts
ಋಕ್ಷರಾಜ	ṛkṣarāja	king of bears
ಋಕ್ಷಪತಿ	ṛkṣapati	lord of bears or stars
ಋಚಾ	ṛcā	Vedic stanza
ಋಗ್ವೇದ	ṛgveda	the Rigveda
ಋಗ್ವಿಧಾನ	ṛgvidhāna	ritual application of Rigvedic verses
ಋಗ್ವೇದಿನ್	ṛgvedin	knower or follower of the Rigveda
ಋತ್ವಿಜ್	ṛtvij	officiating priest
ಋತ್ವಿಜ್ಯ	ṛtvijya	priestly office or ritual service
ಋತುಚಕ್ರ	ṛtucakra	cycle of seasons
ಋತುಚಾರ್ಯೆ	ṛtucaryā	seasonal regimen
ಋತುಧರ್ಮ	ṛtudharma	seasonal or periodic order
ಋತುಸಂಧಿ	ṛtusandhi	transition between seasons
ಋತುಜ	ṛtuja	seasonally produced
ಋತುಕಾಲ	ṛtukāla	proper season or appointed time
ಋತುಪರಿವರ್ತನ	ṛtuparivartana	seasonal change
ಋತುಯಜ್ಞ	ṛtuyajña	seasonal sacrifice
ಋತುಲಕ್ಷಣ	ṛtulakṣaṇa	characteristic of a season
ಋತುಶಾಸ್ತ್ರ	ṛtuśāstra	study or doctrine of seasons
ಋಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ	ṛṣivākya	statement of a sage
ಋಷಿಮತ	ṛṣimata	opinion or doctrine of a sage
ಋಷಿಪರಂಪರೆ	ṛṣiparamparā	lineage of sages
ಋಷಿಧರ್ಮ	ṛṣidharma	duty or way of a sage
ಋಷಿಕುಲ	ṛṣikula	family or school of a sage
ಋಷಿಕಲ್ಪ	ṛṣikalpa	sage-like
ಋಷಿಪ್ರೋಕ್ತ	ṛṣiprokta	taught or declared by a sage
ಋಷಿಸ್ಮೃತಿ	ṛṣismṛti	remembered teaching of a sage
ಋಷಿಸಂವಾದ	ṛṣisaṃvāda	dialogue involving sages
ಋಷ್ಯಾಶ್ರಮ	ṛṣyāśrama	hermitage of a sage
ಋಜುತ್ವ	ṛjutva	straightness, honesty
ಋಜುತೆ	ṛjutā	uprightness
ಋಜುಮಾರ್ಗ	ṛjumārga	straight or honest path
ಋಜುಬುದ್ಧಿ	ṛjubuddhi	straightforward intellect
ಋಜುಭಾವ	ṛj bhāva	sincerity, straightforward disposition
ಋಜುವಚನ	ṛjuvacana	honest or direct speech
ಋಜುನೀತಿ	ṛjunīti	upright policy
ಋಜುಸ್ವಭಾವ	ṛjusvabhāva	straightforward nature
ಋತಧರ್ಮ	ṛtadharma	duty grounded in cosmic order
ಋತಜ್ಞಾನ	ṛtajñāna	knowledge of truth or cosmic order
ಋತಪಥ	ṛtapatha	path of truth or order
ಋತವಾಕ್ಯ	ṛtavākya	truthful statement
ಋತಸತ್ಯ	ṛtasatya	cosmic and expressed truth

However, this list must be interpreted carefully: ಋತುಚಕ್ರ, ಋಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ, ಋಜುತ್ವ, etc. are distinct complete words, but they still belong to productive families beginning with ಋತು-, ಋಷಿ-, ಋಜು-. If by “fully different” you mean 50 unrelated root words, Sanskrit probably does not supply 50 common ones beginning with ಋ.

Meaningful pairings with varied second words

Below are combinations where the second component is not simply another form of ಋಣ. These are graded for naturalness.

Stronger or readily interpretable combinations

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ
 Debt to one’s father or ancestors.

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಷಿ → ಪಿತೄಷಿ
 An ancestral sage or a sage regarded as an ancestor.
 Grammatically transparent, though not a common Kannada word.

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಕ್ → ಪಿತೄಕ್
 A Vedic verse addressed to the ancestors.
 A clearer conventional expression may be ಪಿತೃಸೂಕ್ತ.

ಪಿತೃ + ಋತು → ಪಿತೄತು
 A season or appointed time connected with ancestor rites.
 Semantically possible, but ಪಿತೃಕಾಲ or ಪಿತೃಪಕ್ಷ is more conventional.

ಮಾತೃ + ಋಣ → ಮಾತೄಣ
 Debt or obligation to one’s mother.

ಮಾತೃ + ಋಷಿ → ಮಾತೄಷಿ
 A female or maternal sage.
 Constructible, but not an ordinary lexical item.

ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಜುತ್ವ → ಕರ್ತೄಜುತ್ವ
 Uprightness of an agent or doer.

ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋಜುತೆ → ಕರ್ತೄಜುತೆ
 Honesty or straightforwardness of the responsible agent.

ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ಕರ್ತೄತಧರ್ಮ
 The duty of an agent in accordance with cosmic or moral order.

ಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತಜ್ಞಾನ → ಕರ್ತೄತಜ್ಞಾನ
 A doer’s knowledge of right order or truth.

ವಕ್ತೃ + ಋಜುತ್ವ → ವಕ್ತೄಜುತ್ವ
 Directness or honesty of a speaker.

ವಕ್ತೃ + ಋಜುವಚನ → ವಕ್ತೄಜುವಚನ
 Straightforward speech by a speaker.

ವಕ್ತೃ + ಋತವಾಕ್ಯ → ವಕ್ತೄತವಾಕ್ಯ
 A speaker’s statement conforming to truth.

ಶಾಸ್ತೃ + ಋಜುನೀತಿ → ಶಾಸ್ತೄಜುನೀತಿ
 Upright policy of a ruler or instructor.

ಶಾಸ್ತೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ಶಾಸ್ತೄತಧರ್ಮ
 A ruler’s or teacher’s duty grounded in right order.

ನೇತೃ + ಋಜುಮಾರ್ಗ → ನೇತೄಜುಮಾರ್ಗ
 The straightforward path of a leader.

ನೇತೃ + ಋಜುನೀತಿ → ನೇತೄಜುನೀತಿ
 Upright or honest leadership policy.

ನೇತೃ + ಋಜುಸ್ವಭಾವ → ನೇತೄಜುಸ್ವಭಾವ
 A leader’s straightforward character.

ನೇತೃ + ಋತಪಥ → ನೇತೄತಪಥ
 A leader’s path of truth or right order.

ದಾತೃ + ಋಜುಭಾವ → ದಾತೄಜುಭಾವ
 Sincerity or straightforward intention of a donor.

ದಾತೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ದಾತೄತಧರ್ಮ
 The proper moral duty of a donor.

ದಾತೃ + ಋತುಜದಾನ → ದಾತೄತುಜದಾನ
 A donor’s seasonally produced gift.
 Grammatical, but stylistically heavy.

ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋಜುನೀತಿ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄಜುನೀತಿ
 Upright policy of a protector.

ರಕ್ಷಿತೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ರಕ್ಷಿತೄತಧರ್ಮ
 A protector’s duty according to right order.

ಪೋಷಯಿತೃ + ಋತುಚಾರ್ಯೆ → ಪೋಷಯಿತೄತುಚಾರ್ಯೆ
 Seasonal regimen prescribed by a caregiver or nourisher.

ವೈದ್ಯಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತುಚಾರ್ಯೆ → ವೈದ್ಯಕರ್ತೄತುಚಾರ್ಯೆ
 Seasonal medical regimen formulated by a practitioner.

ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತ್ವಿಜ್ → ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೄತ್ವಿಜ್
 The officiating priest associated with the performer of a sacrifice.
 Grammatically combinable, but the intended relationship requires context.

ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೃ + ಋತುಯಜ್ಞ → ಯಜ್ಞಕರ್ತೄತುಯಜ್ಞ
 A seasonal sacrifice performed by a ritual agent.

ಹೋತೃ + ಋಕ್ → ಹೋತೄಕ್
 A Vedic verse used or uttered by the Hotṛ priest.

ಹೋತೃ + ಋಚಾ → ಹೋತೄಚಾ
 A Vedic stanza associated with the Hotṛ.

ಹೋತೃ + ಋಗ್ವೇದ → ಹೋತೄಗ್ವೇದ
 The Hotṛ’s relation to the Rigveda.
 Semantically recognizable, but not a normal title for the Rigveda.

ಉದ್ಗಾತೃ + ಋಕ್ → ಉದ್ಗಾತೄಕ್
 A verse associated with a Vedic chanter.

ಉದ್ಗಾತೃ + ಋತುಯಜ್ಞ → ಉದ್ಗಾತೄತುಯಜ್ಞ
 A seasonal sacrifice involving an Udgātṛ chanter.

ಶ್ರೋತೃ + ಋಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ → ಶ್ರೋತೄಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ
 A sage’s statement as received by a listener.

ಶ್ರೋತೃ + ಋಜುಬುದ್ಧಿ → ಶ್ರೋತೄಜುಬುದ್ಧಿ
 Straightforward understanding on the part of a listener.

ಜ್ಞಾತೃ + ಋತಜ್ಞಾನ → ಜ್ಞಾತೄತಜ್ಞಾನ
 A knower’s knowledge of cosmic truth.

ಜ್ಞಾತೃ + ಋಷಿಮತ → ಜ್ಞಾತೄಷಿಮತ
 A knower’s understanding of a sage’s doctrine.

ವೇತ್ತೃ + ಋಗ್ವೇದ → ವೇತ್ತೄಗ್ವೇದ
 The Rigveda as known by an expert.
 The natural Sanskrit expression would more likely be ಋಗ್ವೇದವೇತ್ತೃ, with the components reversed.

ವೇತ್ತೃ + ಋತುಶಾಸ್ತ್ರ → ವೇತ್ತೄತುಶಾಸ್ತ್ರ
 A specialist’s knowledge of seasonal science.
 Again, ಋತುಶಾಸ್ತ್ರವೇತ್ತೃ is the more natural compound order.

ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ + ಋತ → ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೄತ
 A creator associated with cosmic order.

ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ಸ್ರಷ್ಟೄತಧರ್ಮ
 The creator’s principle of cosmic order.

ವಿಧಾತೃ + ಋತ → ವಿಧಾತೄತ
 An ordainer connected with cosmic order.

ವಿಧಾತೃ + ಋತುಚಕ್ರ → ವಿಧಾತೄತುಚಕ್ರ
 The ordainer’s cycle of seasons.

ಭರ್ತೃ + ಋತುಧರ್ಮ → ಭರ್ತೄತುಧರ್ಮ
 A maintainer’s seasonal or periodic duty.

ಧರ್ತೃ + ಋತಧರ್ಮ → ಧರ್ತೄತಧರ್ಮ
 The duty of one who sustains cosmic order.

ತ್ರಾತೃ + ಋಜುಮಾರ್ಗ → ತ್ರಾತೄಜುಮಾರ್ಗ
 The straightforward path of a protector or saviour.

ಗೋಪ್ತೃ + ಋತಪಥ → ಗೋಪ್ತೄತಪಥ
 A guardian’s path of right order.

ಪ್ರಶಾಸ್ತೃ + ಋಜುನೀತಿ → ಪ್ರಶಾಸ್ತೄಜುನೀತಿ
 An administrator’s upright policy.

ನಿಯಂತೃ + ಋತುಚಕ್ರ → ನಿಯಂತೄತುಚಕ್ರ
 A regulator or controller of the seasonal cycle.

ಆಖ್ಯಾತೃ + ಋಷಿಸಂವಾದ → ಆಖ್ಯಾತೄಷಿಸಂವಾದ
 A narrator’s account of a dialogue between sages.

The crucial finding

Although the sandhi in all these can be mechanically applied, many resulting expressions do not sound natural, because Sanskrit normally chooses the semantically determining element first.

For example:

ವೇತ್ತೃ + ಋಗ್ವೇದ → ವೇತ್ತೄಗ್ವೇದ is formally sandhi-capable
But ಋಗ್ವೇದವೇತ್ತೃ, “knower of the Rigveda,” is the natural compound

Likewise:

ಶ್ರೋತೃ + ಋಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ → ಶ್ರೋತೄಷಿವಾಕ್ಯ is constructible
But ಋಷಿವಾಕ್ಯಶ್ರೋತೃ, “listener to a sage’s words,” is more natural

The problem is therefore not the sandhi rule. It is compound semantics and word order.

Honest conclusion

I cannot responsibly provide 50 common or established natural compounds having the pattern:

word ending in ಋ + completely unrelated word beginning in ಋ

Only a small number are likely to be idiomatic. The strongest remains:

ಪಿತೃ + ಋಣ → ಪಿತೄಣ

Other combinations may be:

phonologically valid,
morphologically possible,
semantically interpretable,

but still not be established Kannada or idiomatic Sanskrit words.

Also, there probably are not 50 common, unrelated Sanskrit lexical roots beginning with ಋ. Most available words belong to a few families:

ಋಣ-, debt
ಋತ-, truth or cosmic order
ಋತು-, season
ಋಷಿ-, sage
ಋಕ್/ಋಚ್-, Vedic verse
ಋಜು-, straight or honest
ಋದ್ಧ್-, growth or prosperity
ಋಕ್ಷ-, bear or constellation

The Monier-Williams Sanskrit-English Dictionary entry under ऋ confirms both the specialized nature of the vowel and major initial forms such as ಋತ.

So the strongest scholarly statement is:

ೠ/ೄ is fully systematic and can result from sandhi, but naturally occurring lexical examples are rare because independent words beginning with ಋ are rare and because Sanskrit compound order often prevents an ಋ-final agent noun from appearing first.

My previous fifty-item list overstated naturalness by repeatedly extending ಋಣ. Your correction is valid.

We are part of the heritage and should be able to write what Indic syllables exist in other related Indian traditional languages. Cite examples from Veda, and ancient sources of sanskrit also of non sanskrit sources especially Kannada and Tamil. 


Copilot said: ಕೆಳಗಿನ ಲೇಖನವು ಋ ಮತ್ತು ೠ ಅಕ್ಷರಗಳನ್ನು ಮಕ್ಕಳಿಗೆ ಏಕೆ ಕಲಿಸಬೇಕು ಎಂಬುದನ್ನು ಭಾಷಾಶಾಸ್ತ್ರ, ಕನ್ನಡದ ಲಿಪಿಪರಂಪರೆ, ವೇದಸಾಹಿತ್ಯ, ಪ್ರಾಚೀನ ಕನ್ನಡ ವ್ಯಾಕರಣ ಮತ್ತು ತಮಿಳು-ಗ್ರಂಥ ಪರಂಪರೆಯ ಆಧಾರದಲ್ಲಿ ಸಮರ್ಥಿಸುತ್ತದೆ. ವಾದದ ಕೇಂದ್ರಬಿಂದು ಸರಳವಾಗಿದೆ: ಎಲ್ಲ ಅಕ್ಷರಗಳೂ ದಿನನಿತ್ಯದ ಕನ್ನಡ ಪದಗಳಿಗೆ ಮಾತ್ರ ಸೀಮಿತವಲ್ಲ. ಕೆಲವು ಅಕ್ಷರಗಳು ನಮ್ಮ ಲಿಪಿಯ ಐತಿಹಾಸಿಕ ಸಾಮರ್ಥ್ಯವನ್ನೂ ಭಾರತದ ಬಹುಭಾಷಾ ಜ್ಞಾನಪರಂಪರೆಯನ್ನೂ ಉಳಿಸುತ್ತವೆ.

ಋ ಮತ್ತು ೠವನ್ನು ಕನ್ನಡ ಮಕ್ಕಳಿಗೆ ಏಕೆ ಕಲಿಸಬೇಕು?
ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ ಕೇವಲ ಬಳಕೆಯ ಪಟ್ಟಿಯಲ್ಲ, ಒಂದು ಜ್ಞಾನಪರಂಪರೆ

ಮಕ್ಕಳಿಗೆ ಕನ್ನಡ ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆಯನ್ನು ಕಲಿಸುವಾಗ ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯವಾಗಿ ಒಂದು ಪ್ರಶ್ನೆ ಎದುರಾಗುತ್ತದೆ:“ೠ ಅಕ್ಷರದಿಂದ ಯಾವ ಕನ್ನಡ ಪದ ಬರುತ್ತದೆ?” ಸರಳ ಉತ್ತರವೆಂದರೆ, ಇಂದಿನ ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯ ಕನ್ನಡದಲ್ಲಿ ೠದಿಂದ ಆರಂಭವಾಗುವ ಪದಗಳು ಬಹುತೇಕ ಇಲ್ಲ. ಆದರೆ ಅದರಿಂದ ಆ ಅಕ್ಷರ ಅನಗತ್ಯವಾಗುವುದಿಲ್ಲ. ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾಲೆ ಎನ್ನುವುದು ದಿನನಿತ್ಯದಲ್ಲಿ ಹೆಚ್ಚಾಗಿ ಬಳಸುವ ಶಬ್ದಗಳ ಪಟ್ಟಿಯಷ್ಟೇ ಅಲ್ಲ. ಒಂದು ಲಿಪಿ ಯಾವ ಯಾವ ಭಾಷಾಧ್ವನಿಗಳನ್ನು ದಾಖಲಿಸಬಲ್ಲದು ಎಂಬುದನ್ನು ತೋರಿಸುವ ವ್ಯವಸ್ಥೆಯೂ ಹೌದು.

ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿಯನ್ನು ಇತಿಹಾಸದಲ್ಲಿ ಕನ್ನಡಕ್ಕಷ್ಟೇ ಬಳಸಿಲ್ಲ. ಕರ್ನಾಟಕದಲ್ಲಿ ಸಂಸ್ಕೃತ, ತುಳು, ಕೊಂಕಣಿ, ಕೊಡವ, ಸಂಕೇತಿ ಮತ್ತು ಇತರ ಭಾಷೆಗಳನ್ನೂ ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿಯಲ್ಲಿ ಬರೆಯಲಾಗಿದೆ. ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿ ಬ್ರಾಹ್ಮೀ ಮೂಲದ ಅಬುಗಿಡಾ ವ್ಯವಸ್ಥೆಯಾಗಿದ್ದು, ಸ್ವತಂತ್ರ ಸ್ವರ ಮತ್ತು ವ್ಯಂಜನಕ್ಕೆ ಸೇರುವ ಸ್ವರಚಿಹ್ನೆ ಎರಡನ್ನೂ ಬಳಸುತ್ತದೆ. ಆದ್ದರಿಂದ ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿಯ ಅಕ್ಷರಸಂಪತ್ತನ್ನು ಅರ್ಥಮಾಡಿಕೊಳ್ಳುವಾಗ ಕನ್ನಡ ಭಾಷೆ ಮತ್ತು ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿ ಎರಡನ್ನೂ ಪ್ರತ್ಯೇಕವಾಗಿ ನೋಡಬೇಕು.

ಈ ದೃಷ್ಟಿಯಿಂದ ಋ ಮತ್ತು ೠವು ಕನ್ನಡದ ಅನ್ಯ ಅಕ್ಷರಗಳಲ್ಲ. ಅವು ಕನ್ನಡ ಲಿಪಿಯಲ್ಲಿರುವ, ಮುಖ್ಯವಾಗಿ ಸಂಸ್ಕೃತ ಮತ್ತು ಭಾರತೀಯ ಶಾಸ್ತ್ರೀಯ ಪಠ್ಯಗಳನ್ನು ನಿಖರವಾಗಿ ಬರೆಯಲು ಬೇಕಾಗುವ ಸ್ವರಗಳು.

ಋ ಮತ್ತು ೠ ಯಾವ ಧ್ವನಿಗಳನ್ನು ಸೂಚಿಸುತ್ತವೆ?

ಋ ಸಂಸ್ಕೃತದ vocalic r, ಅಂದರೆ ಸ್ವರದ ಕೆಲಸ ಮಾಡುವ “ರ್” ಧ್ವನಿ. ಇದನ್ನು ರೋಮನ್ ಲಿಪಿಯಲ್ಲಿ ṛ ಎಂದು ಬರೆಯುತ್ತಾರೆ. ಅದರ ದೀರ್ಘ ರೂಪ ೠ, ಇದನ್ನು ṝ ಎಂದು ಬರೆಯುತ್ತಾರೆ.

ಸ್ವತಂತ್ರ ಸ್ವರ	ವ್ಯಂಜನಕ್ಕೆ ಸೇರುವ ಚಿಹ್ನೆ	ಲಿಪ್ಯಂತರ	ವಿವರಣೆಋ	ೃ	ṛ	ಹ್ರಸ್ವ ಋ
ೠ	ೄ	ṝ	ದೀರ್ಘ ಋ

ಉದಾಹರಣೆಗೆ:

ಕ + ೃ = ಕೃ
ಕ + ೄ = ಕೄ
ತ + ೃ = ತೃ
ತ + ೄ = ತೄ

ಋ ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯ “ರು” ಅಥವಾ “ರಿ”ಯಂತೆಯೇ ಅಲ್ಲ. ಪ್ರಾಚೀನ ಸಂಸ್ಕೃತ ಧ್ವನಿವ್ಯವಸ್ಥೆಯಲ್ಲಿ “ರ್” ಸ್ವತಃ ಒಂದು ಅಕ್ಷರಮಾತ್ರೆಯ ಕೇಂದ್ರವಾಗಿ ಕೆಲಸ ಮಾಡುತ್ತದೆ. ಆಧುನಿಕ ದಕ್ಷಿಣ ಭಾರತೀಯ ಉಚ್ಚಾರಣೆಯಲ್ಲಿ ಅದು ಅನೇಕ ಬಾರಿ “ರು”ಗೆ ಸಮೀಪಿಸುತ್ತದೆ. ಅದಕ್ಕಾಗಿ ಕೃಷ್ಣವನ್ನು ಸಾಮಾನ್ಯ ಕನ್ನಡ ಮಾತಿನಲ್ಲಿ “ಕ್ರುಷ್ಣ” ಅಥವಾ “ಕೃಷ್ಣ”ಕ್ಕೆ ಸಮೀಪವಾಗಿ ಉಚ್ಚರಿಸಲಾಗುತ್ತದೆ. ಆದರೆ ಲಿಪಿಯಲ್ಲಿರುವ ೃ ಮೂಲ ಪದದ ಧ್ವನಿಚರಿತ್ರೆಯನ್ನು ಉಳಿಸುತ್ತದೆ.

ಋ ಈಗಾಗಲೇ ಕನ್ನಡ ಜೀವನದ ಭಾಗವಾಗಿದೆ

ಋ ಕನ್ನಡಕ್ಕೆ ಸಂಪೂರ್ಣ ಹೊರಗಿನ ಧ್ವನಿ ಎಂದು ಹೇಳಲು ಸಾಧ್ಯವಿಲ್ಲ. ಋ ಅಥವಾ ೃ ಹೊಂದಿರುವ ಅನೇಕ ಪದಗಳು ಕನ್ನಡದ ಧಾರ್ಮಿಕ, ವೈಜ್ಞಾನಿಕ, ಸಾಹಿತ್ಯಿಕ ಮತ್ತು ದೈನಂದಿನ ಪದಕೋಶದಲ್ಲಿ ನೆಲೆಗೊಂಡಿವೆ:

ಋತು
ಋಷಿ
